<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Eisenbud–Green–Harris and lex-plus-powers conjectures over every characteristic-zero field for homogeneous regular sequences of any positive length, with degrees at least two. For every homogeneous ideal containing such a sequence, the corresponding lex-plus-powers ideal has the same Hilbert function and at least as large a graded Betti number in every homological and internal degree.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $n\ge1$, every ordered degree sequence $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$, and every homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ containing a regular sequence of these degrees, the spaces in <a href="#eq:prescription" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prescription">[eq:prescription]</a> exist and $J=\bigoplus_{d\ge0}J_d$ is a homogeneous ideal. For this single ideal, $$\beta^S_{p,j}(S/I)\le\beta^S_{p,j}(S/J) \qquad\text{for every \(p\ge0\) and \(j\in\mathbb Z\)}.$$ There is no restriction on the degrees or number of the additional generators of $I$.
  - Explanation: The prescribed lex-plus-powers ideal simultaneously preserves the Hilbert function and bounds every graded quotient Betti number over the complex numbers.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero)): Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, let $$S_F=F[x_1,\ldots,x_n],\qquad 1\le c\le n,\qquad 2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_c, \qquad P_F=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_c^{a_c}),$$ with the standard grading and lexicographic order $x_1>\cdots>x_n$. Suppose that a homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_c$ with $\deg f_i=a_i$. For $d\ge0$, put $$q_d=\dim_F I_d-\dim_F(P_F)_d.$$ This integer lies between zero and the number of degree-$d$ monomials outside $P_F$. Let $J_d$ be the sum of $(P_F)_d$ and the span of the $q_d$ lexicographically greatest such monomials. Then $J=\bigoplus_{d\ge0}J_d$ is a homogeneous ideal, and for this single ideal $$\dim_F(S_F/I)_d=\dim_F(S_F/J)_d\quad(d\ge0),\qquad \beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/I)\le\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/J) \quad(p\ge0,\ j\in\mathbb Z).$$ There is no restriction on the degrees or number of the additional generators of $I$.
  - Explanation: Published Artinian reductions and coefficient-field descent extend the Hilbert and Betti comparison to arbitrary characteristic-zero fields and positive regular-sequence lengths.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-forms)): Let $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, of degrees $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$. There are a field extension $k/\mathbb C$, a central division $k$-algebra $\Delta$, and a commutative graded $k$-subalgebra $$k[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\subseteq A\subseteq N=\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$$ generated by finitely many elements of degree one, containing $t_1,\ldots,t_n\in A_1$, such that $$\label{eq:triangular-forms} t_h^{a_h}=c_h f_h+\sum_{\ell<h}B_{h\ell}f_\ell +\sum_{\ell>h}C_{h\ell}t_\ell \qquad(1\le h\le n).$$ Here $c_h\in k^\times$, $B_{h\ell},C_{h\ell}\in A$, and every summand has degree $a_h$. Moreover, $$\{t^\alpha=t_1^{\alpha_1}\cdots t_n^{\alpha_n}: \alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge 0}^n\}$$ is a graded left-$\Delta$ basis of the *full* algebra $N$.
  - Explanation: The companion construction supplies commuting degree-one forms with triangular power identities and a basis of the full division-coefficient polynomial algebra.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-filt-minimal)): Every finitely generated graded $A$-module $B$ has a graded free resolution whose differentials have entries in $A_{>0}$. In such a resolution, the number of free generators of degree $j$ in homological position $p$ is $\beta^A_{p,j}(B)$. If $H\subsetneq A$ is a homogeneous ideal, then $$\beta^A_{p+1,j}(A/H)=\beta^A_{p,j}(H) \qquad(p\geq 0,\ j\in\mathbb Z). \label{filt:ideal-quotient}$$ If $H$ is monomial, a minimal resolution of $A/H$ can be chosen homogeneous for the exponent multigrading, with all generator multidegrees in $\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^r$.
  - Explanation: Minimal graded resolutions identify free generator counts with graded Tor dimensions and relate proper ideals to their quotients.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-filt-koszul)): Let $K_\bullet(B)$ be the Koszul complex on $y_1,\ldots,y_r$ with coefficients in $B$. Its term of internal degree $j$ in position $p$ is $$K_p(B)_j=\bigoplus_{\substack{\sigma\subseteq\{1,\ldots,r\}\\ \|\sigma\|=p}} B_{j-p}e_\sigma,$$ where $e_\sigma$ has degree $p$. Its homology satisfies $$\dim_F H_p(K_\bullet(B)_j)=\beta^A_{p,j}(B).$$
  - Explanation: Graded Koszul homology computes the graded Betti numbers.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-filt-initial)): With $A,V,E,W$ and $\prec$ as above, for every $d\in\mathbb Z$, every $p\geq0$, and every $j\in\mathbb Z$ one has $$\begin{aligned} \dim_F(E/W)_d &=\dim_F(E/\operatorname{in}_{\prec}W)_d, \label{filt:hilbert}\\ \beta^A_{p,j}(E/W) &\leq\beta^A_{p,j}(E/\operatorname{in}_{\prec}W). \label{filt:betti} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Passing to the leading submodule preserves graded dimensions and cannot decrease graded Betti numbers.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mono-transfer)): Let $S=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, let $k$ be a field containing $\mathbb C$, and let $\Delta$ be a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra. Put $m=\dim_k\Delta$ and $N=\Delta[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, where the $x_i$ are central. Suppose that $t_1,\ldots,t_n\in N_1$ commute and that $$N=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^n}\Delta t^\alpha \label{mono:basis}$$ is a graded direct-sum decomposition. Give $T=k[y_1,\ldots,y_n]$ a right action on $N$ by multiplication, $u\cdot y_i=ut_i$. For every homogeneous ideal $I\subseteq S$, the right $T$-module $Q=N/IN$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \dim_k Q_d&=m\dim_\mathbb C(S/I)_d &&(d\in\mathbb Z),\label{mono:dimension-transfer}\\ \beta^T_{p,j}(Q)&=m\beta^S_{p,j}(S/I) &&(p\geq0,\ j\in\mathbb Z). \label{mono:betti-transfer} \end{aligned}$$ Here right and left modules over the commutative ring $T$ are identified when computing $\mathop{\mathrm{Tor}}$.
  - Explanation: The division-coefficient bimodule transfers a minimal resolution and scales Hilbert dimensions and Betti numbers by the same finite factor.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mono-bound)): Let $S=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, let $2\leq a_1\leq\cdots\leq a_n$, and let $I\subseteq S$ be a homogeneous ideal containing a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_n$ with $\deg f_h=a_h$. Put $P=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_n^{a_n})$. There is a monomial ideal $M\subseteq S$ containing $P$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \dim_\mathbb C(S/M)_d&=\dim_\mathbb C(S/I)_d &&(d\in\mathbb Z),\label{mono:hilbert-bound}\\ \beta^S_{p,j}(S/I)&\leq\beta^S_{p,j}(S/M) &&(p\geq0,\ j\in\mathbb Z). \label{mono:betti-bound} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Leading coefficient spaces produce a monomial ideal containing the prescribed powers with the same Hilbert function and no smaller graded Betti numbers.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-distraction)): For every monomial ideal $K\subseteq S$, its vector-space image $D(K)$ is a homogeneous ideal. Moreover, $$\begin{gathered} \dim_\mathbb C(S/D(K))_d=\dim_\mathbb C(S/K)_d \quad(d\in\mathbb Z),\\ \beta^S_{p,j}(S/D(K))=\beta^S_{p,j}(S/K) \quad(p\ge0,\ j\in\mathbb Z). \end{gathered}$$ The map also satisfies $D(P)=P$.
  - Explanation: The nested-factor distraction preserves the Hilbert function, graded Betti numbers, and the prescribed powers.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stab-main)): Let $S=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$, let $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_n$, and put $P=(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_n^{a_n})$. For every monomial ideal $K\supseteq P$, there is a monomial ideal $H\supseteq P$ stable in the box such that $$\dim_\mathbb C(S/H)_d=\dim_\mathbb C(S/K)_d\quad(d\in\mathbb Z)$$ and $$\beta^S_{p,j}(S/K)\le\beta^S_{p,j}(S/H) \qquad(p\ge0,\ j\in\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: Repeated distractions and lexicographic initial ideals terminate at a box-stable ideal while preserving the Hilbert function and increasing Betti numbers weakly.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-box-inequalities)): Let $r\ge1$, let $b_1,\ldots,b_r$ be positive integers, and let $d\in\mathbb Z$. If $X\subseteq\mathcal B_r(d)$ is stable and $Y=\operatorname{Lex}(X)$, then $\partial Y$ is a lex segment and $$\label{box:three-inequalities} \|\partial X\|\ge\|\partial Y\|, \qquad \|X(0)\|\ge\|Y(0)\|, \qquad \|X(b_r)\|\le\|Y(b_r)\|.$$
  - Explanation: For stable subsets, lex replacement decreases the bounded shadow and zero slice weakly and increases the top slice weakly.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-box-ideal)): Let $b_1,\ldots,b_r$ be positive integers, and let $H\subseteq\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_r]$ be a monomial ideal containing $P_r=(x_1^{b_1+1},\ldots,x_r^{b_r+1})$ and stable in the box sense. Then the subspace $\Lambda H$ defined in <a href="#box:lex-replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="box:lex-replacement">[box:lex-replacement]</a> is a monomial ideal containing $P_r$. It is stable in the box sense and has the same Hilbert function as $H$.
  - Explanation: The bounded-shadow comparison ensures that degreewise lex replacement is an ideal with the same Hilbert function.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-tor-deficit)): For every $p\ge0$ and $j\in\mathbb Z$, the quantity $D^R_{p,j}(B)$ is the sum of the ranks of the two Koszul differentials adjacent to $K^R_p(B)_j$. If $U\subseteq B$ is a graded submodule and $V$ is a graded quotient of $B$, then $$D^R_{p,j}(U)\le D^R_{p,j}(B),\qquad D^R_{p,j}(V)\le D^R_{p,j}(B).$$ The quantity is additive on finite direct sums, satisfies $$D^R_{p,j}(B(-s))=D^R_{p,j-s}(B),$$ and vanishes when $R_{>0}B=0$.
  - Explanation: The Koszul rank deficit is the sum of adjacent differential ranks and decreases weakly under submodules and quotients.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tor-stable)): Let $r\ge1$, let $2\le a_1\le\cdots\le a_r$, and let $A=\mathbb C[x_1,\ldots,x_r]$. Suppose that $H\subseteq A$ is a monomial ideal containing $(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_r^{a_r})$ and is stable in the box. Let $G=\Lambda H$ be its degreewise lex replacement inside the box, together with the prescribed pure powers. Then, for every $p\ge0$ and $j\in\mathbb Z$, $$\beta^A_{p,j}(H)\le\beta^A_{p,j}(G), \qquad \beta^A_{p,j}(A/H)\le\beta^A_{p,j}(A/G).$$
  - Explanation: Endpoint comparisons and induction show that box lex replacement bounds the graded Betti numbers of both ideals and quotients.

Then $J=\\bigoplus_{d\\ge0}J_d$ is a homogeneous ideal, and for this single ideal $$\\dim_F(S_F/I)_d=\\dim_F(S_F/J)_d\\quad(d\\ge0),\\qquad\n \\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/I)\\le\\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/J)\n \\quad(p\\ge0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ There is no restriction on the degrees or number of the additional generators of $I$.",
          "source_id": "PM-200.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, let $$S_F=F[x_1,\\ldots,x_n],\\qquad\n 1\\le c\\le n,\\qquad 2\\le a_1\\le\\cdots\\le a_c,\n \\qquad P_F=(x_1^{a_1},\\ldots,x_c^{a_c}),$$ with the standard grading and lexicographic order $x_1>\\cdots>x_n$. Suppose that a homogeneous ideal $I\\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\\ldots,f_c$ with $\\deg f_i=a_i$. For $d\\ge0$, put $$q_d=\\dim_F I_d-\\dim_F(P_F)_d.$$ This integer lies between zero and the number of degree-$d$ monomials outside $P_F$. Let $J_d$ be the sum of $(P_F)_d$ and the span of the $q_d$ lexicographically greatest such monomials. Then $J=\\bigoplus_{d\\ge0}J_d$ is a homogeneous ideal, and for this single ideal $$\\dim_F(S_F/I)_d=\\dim_F(S_F/J)_d\\quad(d\\ge0),\\qquad\n \\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/I)\\le\\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/J)\n \\quad(p\\ge0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ There is no restriction on the degrees or number of the additional generators of $I$.",
      "explanation": "Published Artinian reductions and coefficient-field descent extend the Hilbert and Betti comparison to arbitrary characteristic-zero fields and positive regular-sequence lengths.",

        {

Suppose that a homogeneous ideal $I\\subseteq S_F$ contains a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\\ldots,f_c$ with $\\deg f_i=a_i$. For $d\\ge0$, put $$q_d=\\dim_F I_d-\\dim_F(P_F)_d.$$ This integer lies between zero and the number of degree-$d$ monomials outside $P_F$. Let $J_d$ be the sum of $(P_F)_d$ and the span of the $q_d$ lexicographically greatest such monomials. Then $J=\\bigoplus_{d\\ge0}J_d$ is a homogeneous ideal, and for this single ideal $$\\dim_F(S_F/I)_d=\\dim_F(S_F/J)_d\\quad(d\\ge0),\\qquad\n \\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/I)\\le\\beta^{S_F}_{p,j}(S_F/J)\n \\quad(p\\ge0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ There is no restriction on the degrees or number of the additional generators of $I$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-forms",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,\\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, of degrees $2\\le a_1\\le\\cdots\\le a_n$. There are a field extension $k/\\mathbb C$, a central division $k$-algebra $\\Delta$, and a commutative graded $k$-subalgebra $$k[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]\\subseteq A\\subseteq N=\\Delta[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$$ generated by finitely many elements of degree one, containing $t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\in A_1$, such that $$\\label{eq:triangular-forms}\n t_h^{a_h}=c_h f_h+\\sum_{\\ell<h}B_{h\\ell}f_\\ell\n                  +\\sum_{\\ell>h}C_{h\\ell}t_\\ell\n \\qquad(1\\le h\\le n).$$ Here $c_h\\in k^\\times$, $B_{h\\ell},C_{h\\ell}\\in A$, and every summand has degree $a_h$. Moreover, $$\\{t^\\alpha=t_1^{\\alpha_1}\\cdots t_n^{\\alpha_n}:\n       \\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge 0}^n\\}$$ is a graded left-$\\Delta$ basis of the *full* algebra $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-forms",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,\\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, of degrees $2\\le a_1\\le\\cdots\\le a_n$. Moreover, $$\\{t^\\alpha=t_1^{\\alpha_1}\\cdots t_n^{\\alpha_n}:\n       \\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge 0}^n\\}$$ is a graded left-$\\Delta$ basis of the *full* algebra $N$.",
          "source_id": "PM-200.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $f_1,\\ldots,f_n$ be a homogeneous regular sequence in $\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, of degrees $2\\le a_1\\le\\cdots\\le a_n$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-filt-minimal",
        "exact_quote": "Every finitely generated graded $A$-module $B$ has a graded free resolution whose differentials have entries in $A_{>0}$. In such a resolution, the number of free generators of degree $j$ in homological position $p$ is $\\beta^A_{p,j}(B)$. If $H\\subsetneq A$ is a homogeneous ideal, then $$\\beta^A_{p+1,j}(A/H)=\\beta^A_{p,j}(H)\n \\qquad(p\\geq 0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).\n \\label{filt:ideal-quotient}$$ If $H$ is monomial, a minimal resolution of $A/H$ can be chosen homogeneous for the exponent multigrading, with all generator multidegrees in $\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0}^r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-filt-minimal",

          "source_id": "PM-200.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-filt-koszul",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K_\\bullet(B)$ be the Koszul complex on $y_1,\\ldots,y_r$ with coefficients in $B$. Its term of internal degree $j$ in position $p$ is $$K_p(B)_j=\\bigoplus_{\\substack{\\sigma\\subseteq\\{1,\\ldots,r\\}\\\\\n                              |\\sigma|=p}} B_{j-p}e_\\sigma,$$ where $e_\\sigma$ has degree $p$. Its homology satisfies $$\\dim_F H_p(K_\\bullet(B)_j)=\\beta^A_{p,j}(B).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-200.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-filt-initial",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"filt:comparison\" label=\"filt:comparison\"></span> With $A,V,E,W$ and $\\prec$ as above, for every $d\\in\\mathbb Z$, every $p\\geq0$, and every $j\\in\\mathbb Z$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dim_F(E/W)_d\n    &=\\dim_F(E/\\operatorname{in}_{\\prec}W)_d,\n       \\label{filt:hilbert}\\\\\n \\beta^A_{p,j}(E/W)\n    &\\leq\\beta^A_{p,j}(E/\\operatorname{in}_{\\prec}W).\n       \\label{filt:betti}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-200.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-filt-initial",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"filt:comparison\" label=\"filt:comparison\"></span> With $A,V,E,W$ and $\\prec$ as above, for every $d\\in\\mathbb Z$, every $p\\geq0$, and every $j\\in\\mathbb Z$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dim_F(E/W)_d\n    &=\\dim_F(E/\\operatorname{in}_{\\prec}W)_d,\n       \\label{filt:hilbert}\\\\\n \\beta^A_{p,j}(E/W)\n    &\\leq\\beta^A_{p,j}(E/\\operatorname{in}_{\\prec}W).\n       \\label{filt:betti}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-200.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"filt:comparison\" label=\"filt:comparison\"></span> With $A,V,E,W$ and $\\prec$ as above, for every $d\\in\\mathbb Z$, every $p\\geq0$, and every $j\\in\\mathbb Z$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dim_F(E/W)_d\n    &=\\dim_F(E/\\operatorname{in}_{\\prec}W)_d,\n       \\label{filt:hilbert}\\\\\n \\beta^A_{p,j}(E/W)\n    &\\leq\\beta^A_{p,j}(E/\\operatorname{in}_{\\prec}W).\n       \\label{filt:betti}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
      "explanation": "Passing to the leading submodule preserves graded dimensions and cannot decrease graded Betti numbers.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mono-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S=\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, let $k$ be a field containing $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\Delta$ be a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra. Put $m=\\dim_k\\Delta$ and $N=\\Delta[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, where the $x_i$ are central. Suppose that $t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\in N_1$ commute and that $$N=\\bigoplus_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0}^n}\\Delta t^\\alpha\n \\label{mono:basis}$$ is a graded direct-sum decomposition. Give $T=k[y_1,\\ldots,y_n]$ a right action on $N$ by multiplication, $u\\cdot y_i=ut_i$. For every homogeneous ideal $I\\subseteq S$, the right $T$-module $Q=N/IN$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dim_k Q_d&=m\\dim_\\mathbb C(S/I)_d\n          &&(d\\in\\mathbb Z),\\label{mono:dimension-transfer}\\\\\n \\beta^T_{p,j}(Q)&=m\\beta^S_{p,j}(S/I)\n          &&(p\\geq0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).\n          \\label{mono:betti-transfer}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here right and left modules over the commutative ring $T$ are identified when computing $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-200.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mono-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S=\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, let $k$ be a field containing $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\Delta$ be a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra. Give $T=k[y_1,\\ldots,y_n]$ a right action on $N$ by multiplication, $u\\cdot y_i=ut_i$. For every homogeneous ideal $I\\subseteq S$, the right $T$-module $Q=N/IN$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dim_k Q_d&=m\\dim_\\mathbb C(S/I)_d\n          &&(d\\in\\mathbb Z),\\label{mono:dimension-transfer}\\\\\n \\beta^T_{p,j}(Q)&=m\\beta^S_{p,j}(S/I)\n          &&(p\\geq0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).\n          \\label{mono:betti-transfer}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here right and left modules over the commutative ring $T$ are identified when computing $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-200.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $S=\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, let $k$ be a field containing $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\Delta$ be a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra.       "explanation": "The division-coefficient bimodule transfers a minimal resolution and scales Hilbert dimensions and Betti numbers by the same finite factor.",

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mono-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S=\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, let $k$ be a field containing $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\Delta$ be a finite-dimensional central division $k$-algebra. Suppose that $t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\in N_1$ commute and that $$N=\\bigoplus_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0}^n}\\Delta t^\\alpha\n \\label{mono:basis}$$ is a graded direct-sum decomposition.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mono-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S=\\mathbb C[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, let $2\\leq a_1\\leq\\cdots\\leq a_n$, and let $I\\subseteq S$ be a homogeneous ideal containing a homogeneous regular sequence $f_1,\\ldots,f_n$ with $\\deg f_h=a_h$. Put $P=(x_1^{a_1},\\ldots,x_n^{a_n})$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Regular sequence** ([source](#eq:prescription)): Here regularity means that multiplication by $f_i$ is injective on $S/(f_1,\ldots,f_{i-1})$ for each $i$.
- **Central division algebra** ([source](#sec:forms)): A central division algebra over $k$ is a finite-dimensional $k$-algebra with center $k$ in which every nonzero element is invertible.
- **Exponent box** ([source](#sec:stabilization)): For $1\le r\le n$ and $d\in\mathbb Z$, put $$\mathcal B_r(d) =\{u\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^r: \|u\|=d,\ 0\le u_i\le b_i \text{ for }1\le i\le r\}.$$
- **Box stability** ([source](#stmt-stab-definition)): A subset $X\subseteq\mathcal B_r(d)$ is *stable* if $$u\in X,\quad h<\ell,\quad u+e_h-e_\ell\in\mathcal B_r(d) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad u+e_h-e_\ell\in X.$$ A monomial ideal containing $(x_1^{a_1},\ldots,x_r^{a_r})$ is *stable in the box* if its included exponent vectors in $\mathcal B_r(d)$ form a stable set for every $d$. This definition imposes no transfer condition on monomials outside the box.
- **Degreewise lex replacement** ([source](#box:lex-replacement)): Define its degreewise lex replacement by $$\label{box:lex-replacement} (\Lambda H)_d =(P_r)_d+ \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C} \{x^u:u\in\operatorname{Lex}(X_d)\}, \qquad \Lambda H=\bigoplus_{d\ge0}(\Lambda H)_d.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-forms) / [proof](#sec:forms): Commuting division-coefficient forms -> The companion construction supplies commuting degree-one forms with triangular power identities and a basis of the full division-coefficient polynomial algebra.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-filt-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-initial): Filtered graded Koszul comparison -> Passing to the leading submodule preserves graded dimensions and cannot decrease graded Betti numbers.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-mono-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mono-transfer): Minimal resolution transfer -> The division-coefficient bimodule transfers a minimal resolution and scales Hilbert dimensions and Betti numbers by the same finite factor.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-mono-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mono-bound): Leading coefficient spaces -> Leading coefficient spaces produce a monomial ideal containing the prescribed powers with the same Hilbert function and no smaller graded Betti numbers.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-stab-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stab-main): Distractions and lexicographic initial ideals -> Repeated distractions and lexicographic initial ideals terminate at a box-stable ideal while preserving the Hilbert function and increasing Betti numbers weakly.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-box-inequalities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-box-inequalities): Stable-box shadow and slice induction -> For stable subsets, lex replacement decreases the bounded shadow and zero slice weakly and increases the top slice weakly.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-box-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-box-ideal): Degreewise lex replacement -> The bounded-shadow comparison ensures that degreewise lex replacement is an ideal with the same Hilbert function.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-tor-stable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tor-stable): Endpoint Koszul rank induction -> Endpoint comparisons and induction show that box lex replacement bounds the graded Betti numbers of both ideals and quotients.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tor-stable-2): Assembly of Hilbert and Betti comparisons -> The prescribed lex-plus-powers ideal simultaneously preserves the Hilbert function and bounds every graded quotient Betti number over the complex numbers.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tor-stable-3): Artinian reduction and coefficient-field descent -> Published Artinian reductions and coefficient-field descent extend the Hilbert and Betti comparison to arbitrary characteristic-zero fields and positive regular-sequence lengths.

Put $m=\\dim_k\\Delta$ and $N=\\Delta[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$, where the $x_i$ are central. Suppose that $t_1,\\ldots,t_n\\in N_1$ commute and that $$N=\\bigoplus_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0}^n}\\Delta t^\\alpha\n \\label{mono:basis}$$ is a graded direct-sum decomposition. For every homogeneous ideal $I\\subseteq S$, the right $T$-module $Q=N/IN$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dim_k Q_d&=m\\dim_\\mathbb C(S/I)_d\n          &&(d\\in\\mathbb Z),\\label{mono:dimension-transfer}\\\\\n \\beta^T_{p,j}(Q)&=m\\beta^S_{p,j}(S/I)\n          &&(p\\geq0,\\ j\\in\\mathbb Z).\n          \\label{mono:betti-transfer}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here right and left modules over the commutative ring $T$ are identified when computing $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}$.",

Let $G=\\Lambda H$ be its degreewise lex replacement inside the box, together with the prescribed pure powers. Then, for every $p\\ge0$ and $j\\in\\mathbb Z$, $$\\beta^A_{p,j}(H)\\le\\beta^A_{p,j}(G),\n \\qquad\n \\beta^A_{p,j}(A/H)\\le\\beta^A_{p,j}(A/G).$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **bounded-shadow**: Lex replacement of a stable degree-box subset has a lex shadow whose cardinality does not exceed the original shadow cardinality.
- **endpoint-slices**: Lex replacement does not increase the zero-slice cardinality and does not decrease the top-slice cardinality.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **unstable-input**: Apply the endpoint and shadow comparisons to a subset without checking stability. -> The simultaneous endpoint comparisons require stability.
- **wrong-order**: Start the lex replacement with the least monomials. -> The replacement would use the opposite monomial convention.
- **finite-to-general**: Treat finite box checks as certification of the general regular-sequence Betti theorem. -> Box enumeration does not verify the division-coefficient construction or coefficient-field descent.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Degree-box lex replacement and bounded shadows are finite constructions. This implementation checks the shadow and endpoint comparisons for stable subsets; it does not implement the division-coefficient reduction or certify the general Betti inequalities.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with positive integer bounds b and integer degree d.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= len(b) <= 3; each bound <= 2; -1 <= d <= sum(b)+1.
# OUTPUTS: Exact table of all stable subsets, lex replacements, shadows and endpoint counts.
# TIME: O(2^N N r^2 + 2^N N log N), N = degree-box size, r = variable count.
# SPACE: O(2^N N + product(b_i+1)).
# ARITHMETIC: Integer coordinates and certified exact python-flint fmpz counts.
# FAILURE: Reject malformed or out-of-contract input; assertions fail on violated inequalities.
import itertools
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpz

def calculate(data):
    b, d = data['b'], data['d']
    if (not isinstance(b, list) or not 1 <= len(b) <= 3 or
        any(type(t) is not int or not 1 <= t <= 2 for t in b) or
        type(d) is not int or not -1 <= d <= sum(b)+1):
        raise ValueError('outside finite input contract')
    points = list(itertools.product(*(range(t+1) for t in b)))
    box = sorted((u for u in points if sum(u) == d), reverse=True)
    def shadow(chosen):
        result = set()
        for u in chosen:
            for h in range(len(b)):
                if u[h] < b[h]:
                    v = list(u)
                    v[h] += 1
                    result.add(tuple(v))
        return result
    rows = []
    for mask in range(1 << len(box)):
        X = {u for i,u in enumerate(box) if mask & (1 << i)}
        stable = True
        for u in X:
            for h in range(len(b)):
                for ell in range(h+1, len(b)):
                    if u[ell] and u[h] < b[h]:
                        v = list(u)
                        v[h] += 1
                        v[ell] -= 1
                        if tuple(v) not in X:
                            stable = False
        if not stable:
            continue
        Y = set(box[:len(X)])
        sx, sy = shadow(X), shadow(Y)
        count = lambda Z, s: int(fmpz(sum(u[-1] == s for u in Z)))
        counts = [len(sx), len(sy), count(X,0), count(Y,0), count(X,b[-1]), count(Y,b[-1])]
        assert counts[0] >= counts[1] and counts[2] >= counts[3] and counts[4] <= counts[5]
        nextbox = sorted((v for v in points if sum(v) == d+1), reverse=True)
        assert sy == set(nextbox[:len(sy)])
        rows.append([mask, (1 << len(Y))-1] + counts)
    return rows

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(calculate(json.loads(sys.stdin.read())), separators=(',', ':')))
```

<!-- PM:END pm.triptych.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A symbolic algebra implementation can adapt finite box lex replacement to preserve the Hilbert function of a box-stable monomial ideal containing prescribed pure powers. This is an inferred implementation use of the algebraic construction. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No game or simulation model is supplied; homogeneous ideals and regular sequences alone do not establish a game or simulation contract. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A symbolic comparison tool can construct degreewise box lex segments and check their bounded shadows. The construction supports Hilbert-function comparison; it supplies no general software performance guarantee. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The paper defines a finite degreewise lex replacement for a monomial ideal stable in the box and proves that retaining prescribed powers yields an ideal with the same Hilbert function. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Exponent vectors, bounded shadows and slices provide an inferred representation for the finite monomial data used in lex replacement. The paper proves algebraic properties of these sets, without prescribing a storage layout. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns homogeneous ideals and graded invariants. An accelerator mapping and hardware resource contract are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The characteristic-zero ideal comparison supplies no cryptographic construction or security reduction. A cryptographic contract is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source supplies no learning model or statistical guarantee connecting Hilbert functions and graded Betti numbers to prediction. A learning contract is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The algebraic extremality concerns graded Betti numbers at a fixed Hilbert function. An operations-research objective, feasible model and solver contract are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem compares graded invariants of homogeneous ideals. A distributed execution, consistency or communication contract is ABSENT. |
| other (commutative algebra and homological algebra) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | For homogeneous ideals over a characteristic-zero field containing a homogeneous regular sequence of the specified degrees, the prescribed lex-plus-powers ideal preserves the Hilbert function and bounds every graded quotient Betti number. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (counterexample_scope): The comparison for arbitrary regular sequences is asserted over characteristic-zero fields. Blocked use: ['Applying the general comparison in positive characteristic.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The ideal must be homogeneous and contain a homogeneous regular sequence of the prescribed degrees. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to nonhomogeneous constraints.', 'Treating arbitrary generators as a verified regular sequence.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): Regularity requires injectivity modulo the preceding forms. Blocked use: ['Replacing regularity with a count of generators or their degrees.']
- **L4** (edge_case): The Artinian conclusion uses a full-length regular sequence; the characteristic-zero corollary also covers non-Artinian quotients. Blocked use: ['Inferring that an arbitrary shorter regular sequence makes the quotient Artinian.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The degree sequence is ordered and its degrees are at least two. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated regular-sequence comparison outside its degree hypotheses.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): Lex segments begin with the greatest monomials in the specified variable order. Blocked use: ['Using smallest monomials or a different monomial order as the prescribed lex segment.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The shadow and endpoint comparisons require stability inside the bounded exponent box. Blocked use: ['Applying all the endpoint comparisons to arbitrary subsets without checking stability.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Box stability imposes no transfer condition outside the box. Blocked use: ['Requiring unrestricted stability as though it were the definition used here.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Commuting forms need not have commuting individual division coefficients; the proof keeps coefficients on the left and multiplies forms on the right. Blocked use: ['Reordering division coefficients across forms using commutativity of the forms.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): Matching Hilbert functions alone does not establish the Betti comparison. Blocked use: ['Using a Hilbert-function check as a certificate for every graded Betti bound.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): The resolution transfer needs the basis of the full algebra, rather than only the finite complete-intersection quotient. Blocked use: ['Transferring an entire resolution from a basis claim limited to the complete-intersection quotient.']
- **L12** (edge_case): The full-ring ideal is covered separately and all quotient Betti numbers vanish. Blocked use: ['Excluding the full-ring ideal without noting the explicit case.']
- **L13** (unknown): An implementation complexity bound for the general division-coefficient construction is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Claiming a runtime or memory guarantee for constructing the comparison from arbitrary regular-sequence input.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Eisenbud–Green–Harris and lex-plus-powers conjectures over every characteristic-zero field for homogeneous regular sequences of any positive length, with degrees at least two. For every homogeneous ideal containing such a sequence, the corresponding lex-plus-powers ideal has the same Hilbert function and at least as large a graded Betti number in every homological and internal degree.
